54:从基准到最佳实践——在脓毒症护理中降低血培养污染

54 From Benchmark to Best Practice: Reducing Blood Culture Contamination in Sepsis Care
作者： Krier B, Barth J, Huskins W
摘要中文翻译感染预防专家(IP)通常需要在主动接触者追踪与等待阳性检测结果之间进行平衡。研究目标:开发一种工具,使IP能够根据风险等级对疑似麻疹病例进行分层,从而在等待检测结果期间支持优先或延迟主动干预的决策。方法:2025年春季,我们开发了一份初步的矩阵,并利用另外19名疑似患者进一步完善了该工具的评分。研究人群包括在实施期间接受麻疹检测的患者。结果:自实施以来,该矩阵已应用于56名接受麻疹检测的患者。结论:麻疹暴露应对风险矩阵有效地对病例进行了分层,并支持了IP的优先处理工作。方法:我们对库奥特拉河沿岸ARG的发生情况进行了纵向、多地点调查,库奥特拉河是墨西哥莫雷洛斯州的一条城市河流,为资源有限的库奥特拉市提供服务。结果:ARG在整个流域广泛检出,其中四环素耐药基因tetW、头孢菌素酶基因CMY、TEM及多种革兰氏阴性菌的碳青霉烯酶基因(包括KPC、OXA-48和VIM)的丰度尤其高。结论:我们的研究结果表明,在城市河流系统中存在广泛的抗性基因组污染,涵盖环境和临床重要的抗性基因。报告类型:口头报告 学科类别:监测 摘要编号:54 从基准到最佳实践:在败血症护理中降低血培养污染 布拉德·克里尔1,吉恩·巴思2,W·查尔斯·胡斯金斯2 1梅奥诊所医疗系统-曼卡托 2梅奥诊所 背景:麻疹病例需要立即采取行动,在医疗环境中识别并为暴露个体提供预防性治疗。2025年8月,我们部署了最终版工具。该二维风险矩阵...
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在开发一种工具,使感染预防专家(IPs)能够按风险等级对疑似麻疹病例进行分层,从而在等待检测结果期间支持决定优先或延迟主动干预。
设计与对象
研究人群包括实施期间接受麻疹检测的患者,自工具部署以来共计56名接受麻疹检测的患者。
核心方法
研究构建了一份二维风险矩阵,整合流行病学和临床风险因素,每个因素基于“是”或“否”问题进行数值评分。两类评分之和用于分配最终风险等级。采用描述性统计分析风险类别与结局的分布。使用Firth logistic回归评估临床或流行病学风险评分与麻疹诊断的关联。
主要结果
在56名接受麻疹检测的患者中,10.7%(n=6)被归类为高风险,32.1%(n=18)为中风险,57.1%(n=32)为低风险。56名患者中有4例麻疹病例阳性,其中3例归类为高风险,1例为中风险。低风险类别的阴性预测值(NPV)为1,表明所有低风险患者检测结果均为阴性。
解释与创新
麻疹暴露应对风险矩阵有效实现了病例分层,并支持IPs的优先干预决策,将临床与流行病学风险评分从任意分配转变为通过统计分析验证的量化指标。
科研与公卫意义
该工具支持感染预防团队快速开展循证行动,尤其在麻疹发病率上升的环境中,可提高资源分配效率并优先实施干预措施。
局限与证据强度
本研究受限于单一工具应用的依赖性及潜在未测量混杂因素。证据强度为中等,因研究基于单一队列研究且采用统计分析。

54 From Benchmark to Best Practice: Reducing Blood Culture Contamination in Sepsis Care

Authors: Krier B, Barth J, Huskins W
Original abstract (English)
Infection preventionists (IPs) often balance proactive contact tracing with waiting for a positive test result. Objective: To develop a tool that enables IPs to stratify suspect measles cases by risk level, thereby supporting the decision to prioritize or defer active intervention while awaiting test results. Methods: In spring 2025, we developed a preliminary matrix and used 19 additional suspect patients to further refine the scoring of the tool. The study population included patients tested for measles during the implementation period. Results: Since implementa- tion, the matrix was applied to 56 patients tested for measles. Conclusion: The Measles Exposure Response Risk Matrix effectively stratified cases and supported IP prioritization efforts. Methods: We conducted a longi- tudinal, multi-site investigation of ARG occurrence along the Cuautla River, an urban watershed in Morelos, Mexico, serving the resource-lim- ited city of Cuautla. Results: ARGs were widely detected across the watershed, with particularly high abundances of tetW, CMY, TEM, and multiple Gram-negative carbapenemase genes, including KPC, OXA-48, and VIM. Conclusions: Our findings demonstrate extensive resistome contamination within an urban river system, encom- passing both environmental and clinically important ARGs. Charles Huskins2 1Mayo Clinic Health System- Mankato and 2Mayo Clinic Background: A measles case requires immediate action to identify and administer prophylaxis to exposed individuals in healthcare settings. In August 2025, we deployed a finalized tool. The two-dimensional risk matrix...
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to develop a tool for infection preventionists (IPs) to stratify suspect measles cases by risk level, thereby supporting decisions to prioritize or defer active intervention while awaiting test results.
Design and population
The study population included patients tested for measles during the implementation period, with a production cohort of 56 patients tested for measles since the tool's deployment.
Core methods
A two-dimensional risk matrix was developed incorporating both epidemiological and clinical risk factors, each scored numerically based on yes or no questions. The sum of both categories was used to assign a final risk level. Descriptive statistics were used to summarize the distribution of risk categories and outcomes. Firth’s logistic regression was used to evaluate the association between clinical or epidemiological risk score and measles diagnosis.
Main results
Among the 56 patients tested for measles, 10.7% (n=6) were categorized as high risk, 32.1% (n=18) as medium, and 57.1% (n=32) as low risk. Four of the 56 patients were positive for measles, with 3 categorized as high-risk and 1 as medium risk. The low-risk category had an NPV of 1, indicating that all low-risk patients tested negative.
Interpretation and novelty
The Measles Exposure Response Risk Matrix effectively stratified cases and supported IP prioritization efforts, transitioning clinical and epidemiologic risk scores from arbitrary assignment to validated metrics confirmed by statistical analysis.
Scientific and public-health significance
The tool supports rapid, evidence-based actions by infection prevention teams, particularly in settings with rising measles incidence, enabling efficient resource allocation and prioritization of interventions.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its reliance on a single tool's application and the potential for unmeasured confounding factors. The evidence is moderate, as the findings are based on a single cohort and statistical analysis.

亚马孙地区病毒性人兽共患病监测:一体化健康、领土与暴发响应

Vigilância de zoonoses virais na Amazônia: saúde única, território e resposta a surtos
作者： Authors unavailable
摘要中文翻译亚马孙地区病毒性人兽共患病监测采用一体化方法,探讨了地球上生物多样性最丰富的地区之一在病毒性疾病风险、挑战与监测策略方面的问题。该著作讨论了亚马孙作为哨点领土的角色、一体化健康(One Health)方法、社会环境决定因素、人-动物-环境监测、偏远地区诊断以及暴发后首个72小时的响应。同时涵盖了实践应用案例,包括奥罗普切热、COVID-19、猴痘与尼帕病毒。本书面向对流行病学、公共卫生、人兽共患病与健康监测感兴趣的学生、研究人员及专业人士。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究探讨了亚马孙地区病毒性人兽共患病一体化监测的必要性,重点关注高生物多样性地区病毒性疾病的风险、挑战与监测策略。背景部分强调了理解独特生态与社会环境因素对疾病出现与传播的重要性。
设计与对象
本研究为一部著作,讨论了亚马孙作为哨点领土、一体化健康方法、社会环境决定因素以及人-动物-环境监测的挑战。同时涵盖了偏远地区疾病监测与暴发后首个72小时响应的内容。
核心方法
方法部分概述了本研究的实践应用,包括针对特定病毒(如奥罗普切热、COVID-19、猴痘与尼帕病毒)的案例研究,用于阐释所讨论的监测与响应策略。
主要结果
主要结果包括识别影响亚马孙地区病毒性人兽共患病的关键因素(如社会环境决定因素),并讨论了猴痘等疾病的监测与响应策略。
解释与创新
作者将研究发现解释为强调在人兽共患病监测工作中整合人类、动物与环境健康的重要性,并突出了将该方法应用于亚马孙地区独特背景的创新性。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果可为高生物多样性地区病毒性人兽共患病监测与快速响应提供公共卫生策略参考,特别是针对猴痘等疾病。
局限与证据强度
本研究基于摘要,未提供详细数据或统计分析,限制了结论的可信度。由于缺乏具体数值结果,证据强度被评估为中等。

Vigilância de zoonoses virais na Amazônia: saúde única, território e resposta a surtos

Authors: Authors unavailable
Original abstract (English)
Vigilância de zoonoses virais na Amazônia apresenta uma abordagem integrada sobre os riscos, desafios e estratégias de vigilância de doenças virais em um dos territórios de maior biodiversidade do planeta. A obra discute a Amazônia como território sentinela, a abordagem de Saúde Única, os determinantes socioambientais, a vigilância humano–animal–ambiente, o diagnóstico em regiões remotas e a resposta às primeiras 72 horas de surtos. Também aborda aplicações práticas envolvendo febre Oropouche, COVID-19, Mpox e vírus Nipah. Destina-se a estudantes, pesquisadores e profissionais interessados em epidemiologia, saúde pública, zoonoses e vigilância em saúde.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study addresses the need for integrated surveillance of viral zoonoses in the Amazon, focusing on the risks, challenges and strategies for monitoring viral diseases in a region of high biodiversity. The background highlights the importance of understanding the unique ecological and socioenvironmental factors that contribute to the emergence and spread of such diseases.
Design and population
The study is a book that discusses the Amazon as a sentinel territory, the One Health approach, socioenvironmental determinants, and the challenges of human–animal–environment surveillance. It also covers the diagnosis of diseases in remote regions and the response to the first 72 hours of outbreaks.
Core methods
The methods section outlines the practical applications of the study, including case studies on specific viruses such as febre Oropouche, COVID-19, Mpox and Nipah virus, which are used to illustrate the surveillance and response strategies discussed.
Main results
The main results include the identification of key factors influencing viral zoonoses in the Amazon, such as socioenvironmental determinants, and the discussion of surveillance and response strategies for diseases like Mpox.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpret the findings as emphasizing the importance of integrating human, animal and environmental health in surveillance efforts, and they highlight the novelty of applying this approach to the unique context of the Amazon region.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings can inform public health strategies for surveillance and rapid response to viral zoonoses in biodiverse regions, particularly in the context of diseases like Mpox.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is based on an abstract and does not provide detailed data or statistical analysis, which limits the confidence in its conclusions. The evidence is considered moderate due to the lack of specific numerical findings.

1970年至2025年全球猴痘疫情动态:对不断演变的传播力和干预效果的系统综述和荟萃分析

Dynamics of global mpox epidemics, 1970-2025: a systematic review and meta-analysis of evolving transmissibility and intervention effects.
作者： Yin-Chien Lin, Tzai-Hung Wen, Wei-Liang Shih, Chia-Wei Liao, Sten H Vermund, Chi-Tai Fang
摘要中文翻译背景:2022年全球猴痘疫情在改良型牛痘病毒-巴伐利亚北(MVA-BN)疫苗广泛部署之前出现下降,这导致人们认为传播在小规模高风险人群中具有自限性。这可能延迟了全球疫苗的分配,并削弱了对自2024年以来主要影响非洲的疫情反弹的应对。2022年疫情下降的原因仍不确定,先前研究对疫苗接种的影响估计差异很大。我们系统综述并荟萃分析了猴痘分支的传播力变化以及干预措施的效果。方法:我们于2025年5月18日通过GenBank、PubMed和Embase检索了猴痘病毒序列、繁殖数(R)估计值和评估干预效果的建模研究。两名评审员独立评估了资格、提取数据并使用经过验证的工具评估偏倚风险。进行了随机效应荟萃分析。结果:有52项研究报告了R估计值,40项研究评估了干预效果,符合纳入标准。对于I型猴痘分支,合并后的R值从1970年至2017年的0.71(95% CI 0.26至1.17)上升至2023年后Ib/Ia亚分支的1.23(1.12-1.33)(p=0.0303)。对于II型猴痘分支,合并后的R值在2017年至2021年为1.11(0.90-1.32),而在2022年至2023年的IIb亚分支中上升至2.66(2.31-3.00)(p<0.0001)。在2022年IIb亚分支暴发期间,实证建模研究估计,行为改变和疫苗接种共同作用导致猴痘病例显著减少(55%,95% CI 37至73;I2=81.2%),尽管不同环境下的效应大小因行为改变的程度以及疫苗部署的时间和覆盖范围而有所不同。结论:在许多研究环境中,行为改变和疫苗接种可能在2022年猴痘疫情下降中发挥了重要作用。鉴于新亚分支出现与人传人传播力逐步增加相关,有效的猴痘疫情控制需要由文化定制的风险沟通支持的主动、快速和公平的疫苗部署。PROSPERO注册号:CRD420250653072。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述关注1970年至2025年全球猴痘疫情的动态,研究猴痘分支的传播力变化以及干预措施的效果。核心问题是行为改变和疫苗接种是否在控制猴痘疫情,尤其是在2022年疫情及随后非洲的疫情反弹中发挥了重要作用。
证据基础与方法
证据基础包括猴痘病毒序列、繁殖数(R)估计值以及评估干预效果的建模研究。本综述于2025年5月18日通过GenBank、PubMed和Embase进行检索。两名评审员独立评估了资格、提取数据并使用经过验证的工具评估偏倚风险。进行了随机效应荟萃分析以综合研究结果。
共识与结论
分支簇I 猴痘 的汇总再生数(R)从1970-2017年的0.71(95% CI 0.26至1.17)上升至2023年后亚分支簇Ib/Ia的1.23(1.12-1.33)。分支簇II 猴痘 在2017-2021年间的R为1.11(0.90-1.32),而在2022-2023年亚分支簇IIb则升至2.66(2.31-3.00)。实证模型研究估计,行为改变与疫苗接种 共同使2022年亚分支簇IIb 暴发 期间的猴痘 病例减少了55%(95% CI 37至73),但效应规模在不同环境中存在差异。
争议与缺口
2022年猴痘病例下降的原因仍不确定,先前研究对疫苗接种的影响估计差异很大。证据还突出了干预效果的异质性,这受到行为改变程度以及疫苗部署的时间和覆盖范围的影响。对于新亚分支传播力增加的确切机制尚无共识。
科研与实践启示
有效的猴痘疫情控制需要由文化定制的风险沟通支持的主动、快速和公平的疫苗部署。本综述强调需要进一步研究新亚分支传播力增加的机制以及在不同环境中优化干预策略。

Dynamics of global mpox epidemics, 1970-2025: a systematic review and meta-analysis of evolving transmissibility and intervention effects.

Authors: Yin-Chien Lin, Tzai-Hung Wen, Wei-Liang Shih, Chia-Wei Liao, Sten H Vermund, Chi-Tai Fang
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: The 2022 global mpox epidemic declined before modified Vaccinia Ankara-Bavarian Nordic (MVA-BN) vaccines were widely deployed, contributing to the perception that transmission was self-limiting within a small high-risk population. This may have delayed global vaccine allocation and weakened responses to the resurgence that has disproportionately affected Africa since 2024. The reasons for the 2022 decline remain uncertain, and prior studies have reported widely divergent estimates of vaccination impact. We systematically reviewed and meta-analysed the evolving transmissibility of mpox clades and the effects of interventions. METHODS: We searched GenBank, PubMed and Embase through 18 May 2025, for mpox virus sequences, reproduction number (R) estimates and modelling studies evaluating intervention effectiveness. Two reviewers independently assessed eligibility, extracted data and evaluated risk of bias using a validated tool. Random-effects meta-analyses were performed. RESULTS: Fifty-two studies reporting R estimates and 40 studies evaluating intervention effectiveness met eligibility criteria. For clade I mpox, pooled R increased from 0.71 (95% CI 0.26 to 1.17) during 1970-2017 to 1.23 (1.12-1.33) for subclades Ib/Ia after 2023 (p=0.0303). For clade II mpox, pooled R was 1.11 (0.90-1.32) during 2017-2021 and increased to 2.66 (2.31-3.00) for subclade IIb in 2022-2023 (p<0.0001). During the 2022 subclade IIb outbreak, empirical modelling studies estimated that behaviour change and vaccination together were associated with a substantial reduction in mpox cases (55%, 95% CI 37 to 73; I2=81.2%), although effect sizes varied across settings according to the extent of behaviour change and the timing and coverage of vaccine rollout. CONCLUSIONS: Behaviour change and vaccination likely played important roles in the decline of the 2022 mpox epidemic in many studied settings. Given the stepwise increase in human-to-human transmissibility associated with the emergence of new subclades, effective mpox epidemic control requires proactive, rapid and equitable vaccine rollout supported by culturally tailored risk communication. PROSPERO REGISTRATION NUMBER: CRD420250653072.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the dynamics of global mpox epidemics from 1970 to 2025, examining the evolving transmissibility of mpox clades and the effects of interventions. The central question is whether behavior change and vaccination have played significant roles in controlling mpox outbreaks, particularly during the 2022 epidemic and subsequent resurgence in Africa.
Evidence base and method
The evidence base includes mpox virus sequences, reproduction number (R) estimates, and modeling studies evaluating intervention effectiveness. The review searched GenBank, PubMed, and Embase through 18 May 2025. Two reviewers independently assessed eligibility, extracted data, and evaluated risk of bias using a validated tool. Random-effects meta-analyses were performed to synthesize findings.
Consensus and conclusions
The pooled reproduction number (R) for clade I mpox increased from 0.71 (95% CI 0.26 to 1.17) during 1970-2017 to 1.23 (1.12-1.33) for subclades Ib/Ia after 2023. For clade II mpox, R was 1.11 (0.90-1.32) during 2017-2021 and rose to 2.66 (2.31-3.00) for subclade IIb in 2022-2023. Empirical modeling studies estimated that behavior change and vaccination together reduced mpox cases by 55% (95% CI 37 to 73) during the 2022 subclade IIb outbreak, though effect sizes varied by setting.
Controversies and gaps
The reasons for the 2022 decline in mpox cases remain uncertain, with prior studies reporting widely divergent estimates of vaccination impact. The evidence also highlights heterogeneity in intervention effectiveness, influenced by the extent of behavior change and the timing and coverage of vaccine rollout. There is a lack of consensus on the exact mechanisms driving the increased transmissibility of new subclades.
Research and practice implications
Effective mpox epidemic control requires proactive, rapid, and equitable vaccine rollout, supported by culturally tailored risk communication. The review emphasizes the need for further research on the mechanisms of increased transmissibility in new subclades and the optimization of intervention strategies across different settings.

男性同性性行为中猴痘感染的患病率:系统综述和荟萃分析

Prevalence of mpox infection among men who have sex with men: a systematic review and meta-analysis
作者： Luqiu Tao, Yanzheng Zou, Lilei Wang, Li Pan, Hong-ru Zhu, Yili Qian, Yu He, Zhihui Wu, Wei Wang, 潘丽
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述旨在估计全球男性同性性行为者(MSM)人群中猴痘感染的患病率,重点关注性传播的作用以及不同猴痘病毒亚型的影响。
证据基础与方法
The evidence base included 28 studies retrieved from PubMed, Web of Science, Embase, China National Knowledge Infrastructure (CNKI), and Wanfang Data, covering studies published in English or Chinese from inception to 16 December 2024. The review used fixed-effects or random-effects meta-analysis to estimate pooled prevalence, with heterogeneity assessed using Cochran's Q test and I 2 statistics. Meta-regression was conducted to identify potential sources of heterogeneity, and publication bias was evaluated using funnel plots, Egger's regression test, and Begg's rank correlation test. Sensitivity analyses were performed via leave-one-out and stratified validation methods.
共识与结论
The pooled prevalence of mpox among MSM was estimated at 2.30% [95% confidence interval (CI): 1.29–3.57%]. Meta-regression identified continent as the most significant moderator, with South America showing a significantly higher prevalence (9.58, 95% CI: 2.71–20.01%) compared to Asia (1.14, 95% CI: 0.56–1.90%) (P = 0.019). The study concluded that mpox prevalence among MSM is significant but highly heterogeneous, influenced by regional epidemiological contexts and study methodologies.
争议与缺口
综述指出采样方法对异质性有所贡献,但未观察到高风险MSM、无症状MSM或一般MSM人群中患病率的显著统计学差异。关于2024年初Ib亚型病例激增的确切机制尚不明确,且研究未提供关于区域患病率差异的生物或生态因素的详细见解。
科研与实践启示
研究结果强调了需要优先考虑地理区域的干预措施,并标准化监测以实现准确的风险评估和指导未来暴发的资源分配。综述建议未来研究应关注导致MSM人群中猴痘患病率区域差异的流行病学和生态因素,以及不同亚型在性传播动态中的作用。

Prevalence of mpox infection among men who have sex with men: a systematic review and meta-analysis

Authors: Luqiu Tao, Yanzheng Zou, Lilei Wang, Li Pan, Hong-ru Zhu, Yili Qian, Yu He, Zhihui Wu, Wei Wang, 潘丽
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background Mpox has been twice declared as public health emergency of international concern by World Health Organization. The 2022–2024 global outbreak has been primarily driven by sexual transmission within men who have sex with men (MSM) networks, predominantly involving Clade IIb, with a subsequent surge of Clade Ib in early 2024. MSM account for a large proportion of confirmed mpox cases. This review aimed to estimate the prevalence of mpox infection among MSM populations. Methods We searched PubMed, Web of Science, Embase, China National Knowledge Infrastructure (CNKI) and Wanfang Data for studies published in English or Chinese from inception to 16 December 2024. Eligible studies reported mpox cases among MSM with confirmed diagnosis (laboratory or self-reported) during the outbreak period. Reviews, case reports, and studies without case numbers were excluded. American Agency for Healthcare Research and Quality and Newcastle-Ottawa Scale were adopted for quality assessment. Fixed-effects or random-effects meta-analysis was performed to estimate the pooled prevalence of mpox among MSM. Heterogeneity was measured using Cochran's Q test and I 2 statistics. Meta regression analysis was performed to discover the potential source of the heterogeneity. Funnel plot, Egger's regression test and Begg's rank correlation test were conducted to evaluate the publication bias. Sensitivity analyses were performed by leave-one-out analysis and by stratified validation. Results From 1606 retrieved studies, 28 studies were eligible and included, containing a total of 46,598 MSM participants and 1274 mpox cases from 15 countries. The pooled mpox prevalence among MSM population was 2.30% [95% confidence interval ( CI ): 1.29–3.57%]. Meta-regression identified continent as the most significant moderator, with prevalence in South America (9.58, 95% CI : 2.71–20.01%) being significantly higher than in Asia (1.14, 95% CI : 0.56–1.90%) ( P = 0.019). Sampling method also contributed to the heterogeneity, whereas no statistically significant difference was observed in mpox prevalence among high-risk MSM, asymptomatic MSM, comparing to the general MSM populations respectively. Conclusions This study confirms a significant but highly heterogeneous burden of mpox among MSM worldwide. The findings suggest that the observed prevalence is shaped more by regional epidemiological contexts and study methodologies, which emphasizes the necessity for geographically prioritized interventions and standardizing surveillance to enable accurate risk assessment and guide resource allocation in future outbreaks.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review aimed to estimate the prevalence of mpox infection among men who have sex with men (MSM) populations globally, focusing on the role of sexual transmission and the impact of different clades of the mpox virus.
Evidence base and method
The evidence base included 28 studies retrieved from PubMed, Web of Science, Embase, China National Knowledge Infrastructure (CNKI), and Wanfang Data, covering studies published in English or Chinese from inception to 16 December 2024. The review used fixed-effects or random-effects meta-analysis to estimate pooled prevalence, with heterogeneity assessed using Cochran's Q test and I 2 statistics. Meta-regression was conducted to identify potential sources of heterogeneity, and publication bias was evaluated using funnel plots, Egger's regression test, and Begg's rank correlation test. Sensitivity analyses were performed via leave-one-out and stratified validation methods.
Consensus and conclusions
The pooled prevalence of mpox among MSM was estimated at 2.30% [95% confidence interval (CI): 1.29–3.57%]. Meta-regression identified continent as the most significant moderator, with South America showing a significantly higher prevalence (9.58, 95% CI: 2.71–20.01%) compared to Asia (1.14, 95% CI: 0.56–1.90%) (P = 0.019). The study concluded that mpox prevalence among MSM is significant but highly heterogeneous, influenced by regional epidemiological contexts and study methodologies.
Controversies and gaps
The review noted that sampling methods contributed to heterogeneity, but no statistically significant difference in prevalence was observed among high-risk MSM, asymptomatic MSM, or general MSM populations. There is a lack of clarity on the exact mechanisms driving the surge in Clade Ib cases in early 2024, and the study did not provide detailed insights into the biological or ecological factors behind the regional variations in prevalence.
Research and practice implications
The findings highlight the need for geographically prioritized interventions and standardized surveillance to enable accurate risk assessment and guide resource allocation in future outbreaks. The review suggests that future research should focus on understanding the epidemiological and ecological factors contributing to the regional variations in mpox prevalence among MSM, as well as the role of different clades in transmission dynamics.

唾液和咽拭子作为猴痘筛查工具:系统性综述与荟萃分析

Saliva and Oropharyngeal Swabs as Screening Tools for Mpox: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis
作者： Kavithrini Anunadika Gammulle, S. M. Premarathne, P. H. Gammulle, T. D. Bandara, Meghashyam Bhat, Harshala Gammulle
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
始于2020年的全球猴痘 暴发于2024年被宣布为国际关注的突发公共卫生事件。然而,关于使用唾液和/或咽拭子样本检测该病毒的科学证据有限。本综述系统性检索现有文献,评估唾液和/或咽拭子样本作为猴痘检测筛查工具的潜力。
证据基础与方法
最终纳入15项研究,共2,610名参与者,包括唾液和/或咽拭子样本(OPSs)检测出病毒的猴痘感染者(聚合酶链反应确认)。质量评估后,采用对数转换随机效应模型汇总阳性率。
共识与结论
The pooled positivity rate of mpox virus DNA was 76.7% (95% confidence interval [CI], 27.9%–96.6%) in saliva and 69.7% (95% CI, 40.1%–88.8%) in OPSs. The median shedding durations were 19 and 21 d for saliva and OPSs, respectively. The high positivity rates and sustained shedding in saliva and OPSs indicate that these methods can serve as noninvasive, adjunct, early-phase alternatives for rapid mpox detection, supporting enhanced surveillance, outbreak monitoring, and global public health preparedness.
争议与缺口
The high heterogeneity between the studies (I 2 = 99.5%) for both specimen types was addressed through sensitivity analyses.
科研与实践启示
本系统性综述的发现直接关乎临床医生、牙科医生及感染控制专家,以了解唾液和咽拭子样本对猴痘的诊断价值。该知识可助力医疗机构内的筛查、感染控制及患者管理。

Saliva and Oropharyngeal Swabs as Screening Tools for Mpox: A Systematic Review and Meta-analysis

Authors: Kavithrini Anunadika Gammulle, S. M. Premarathne, P. H. Gammulle, T. D. Bandara, Meghashyam Bhat, Harshala Gammulle
Original abstract (English)
Objectives: The global mpox outbreak that began in 2020 was declared a public health emergency of international concern in 2024. However, there is limited scientific evidence regarding the use of salivary and/or oropharyngeal swab samples to detect the virus. In this review, we systematically examined existing literature to evaluate the potential of saliva and/or oropharyngeal swab samples as screening tools for mpox detection. Methods: The final analysis included 15 articles comprising 2,610 participants, including mpox-infected individuals (polymerase chain reaction confirmed) whose saliva and/or oropharyngeal samples (OPSs) tested positive for the virus. After a quality assessment, a random-effects model with logit transformation was used to pool the positivity rates. Results: The pooled positivity rate of mpox virus DNA was 76.7% (95% confidence interval [CI], 27.9%–96.6%) in saliva and 69.7% (95% CI, 40.1%–88.8%) in OPSs. The high heterogeneity between the studies (I 2 = 99.5%) for both specimen types was addressed through sensitivity analyses. The median shedding durations were 19 and 21 d for saliva and OPSs, respectively. Conclusions: The high positivity rates and sustained shedding in saliva and OPSs indicate that these methods can serve as noninvasive, adjunct, early-phase alternatives for rapid mpox detection, supporting enhanced surveillance, outbreak monitoring, and global public health preparedness. Knowledge Transfer Statement: The findings of this systematic review are directly relevant to clinicians, dental surgeons, and infection control specialists in understanding the diagnostic value of saliva and oropharyngeal samples for mpox. This knowledge can aid in screening, infection control, and patient management within health care settings.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The global mpox outbreak that began in 2020 was declared a public health emergency of international concern in 2024. However, there is limited scientific evidence regarding the use of salivary and/or oropharyngeal swab samples to detect the virus. In this review, we systematically examined existing literature to evaluate the potential of saliva and/or oropharyngeal swab samples as screening tools for mpox detection.
Evidence base and method

After a quality assessment, a random-effects model with logit transformation was used to pool the positivity rates.
Consensus and conclusions
The pooled positivity rate of mpox virus DNA was 76.7% (95% confidence interval [CI], 27.9%–96.6%) in saliva and 69.7% (95% CI, 40.1%–88.8%) in OPSs. The median shedding durations were 19 and 21 d for saliva and OPSs, respectively. The high positivity rates and sustained shedding in saliva and OPSs indicate that these methods can serve as noninvasive, adjunct, early-phase alternatives for rapid mpox detection, supporting enhanced surveillance, outbreak monitoring, and global public health preparedness.
Controversies and gaps
The high heterogeneity between the studies (I 2 = 99.5%) for both specimen types was addressed through sensitivity analyses.
Research and practice implications
The findings of this systematic review are directly relevant to clinicians, dental surgeons, and infection control specialists in understanding the diagnostic value of saliva and oropharyngeal samples for mpox. This knowledge can aid in screening, infection control, and patient management within health care settings.

新发传染病(猴痘、尼帕病毒和安第斯山脉):传播、环境生存和消毒的叙述性回顾

Emerging infectious diseases (Mpox, Nipah, and Andes): A narrative review of transmission, environmental survival, and disinfection
作者： David J. Weber, William A. Rutala, April Baller, Brooke E Brewer, Emily E. Sickbert-Bennett, E. Sickbert‐Bennett
摘要中文翻译背景:猴痘、尼帕病毒和安第斯病毒是新出现的传染病,导致与医院 传播 相关的 暴发。方法:这篇叙述性综述总结了 猴痘、尼帕病毒和安第斯病毒 流行病学、临床表现、诊断 和 治疗,重点是环境生存、对防腐剂和消毒剂的敏感性以及感染预防。结果:猴痘、尼帕病毒和安第斯病毒都可以在环境中生存,并且能够间接感染 传播。所有这些病毒都有包膜,对常用的防腐剂和表面消毒剂敏感。结论:快速识别已知或疑似感染这些新出现病毒的患者,并遵守感染预防建议,将预防与医疗保健相关的 传播。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述探讨猴痘病毒(MPXV),包括猴痘疾病、其分支(分支I、分支II、分支Ib)及相关暴发,重点关注医院内传播及医疗环境中的感染预防。
证据基础与方法
本综述综合分析了流行病学、临床特征、诊断、治疗、环境生存能力、对消毒剂和防腐剂的敏感性以及猴痘病毒(MPXV)及其相关正痘病毒的感染预防策略。
共识与结论
猴痘病毒(MPXV)具有环境存活能力,并存在间接传播传播潜力;作为包膜病毒,其对常用消毒剂和表面消毒剂仍然敏感。
争议与缺口
所提供的证据未报告猴痘病毒不同分支在环境存活能力上的异质性,亦未提供按分支或环境条件分层的消毒剂效力对比数据。
科研与实践启示
快速识别疑似猴痘患者并严格遵守感染预防建议,对于预防与猴痘病毒相关的医源性传播至关重要。

Emerging infectious diseases (Mpox, Nipah, and Andes): A narrative review of transmission, environmental survival, and disinfection

Authors: David J. Weber, William A. Rutala, April Baller, Brooke E Brewer, Emily E. Sickbert-Bennett, E. Sickbert‐Bennett
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: Mpox, Nipah virus, and Andes virus are emerging infectious diseases that cause outbreaks associated with nosocomial transmission. METHODS: This narrative review summarizes the Mpox, Nipah virus, and Andes virus epidemiology, clinical manifestations, diagnosis, and treatment with a focus on environmental survival, susceptibility to antiseptics and disinfectants, and infection prevention. RESULTS: Mpox, Nipah virus, and Andes virus all survive in the environment and are capable of indirect transmission. All these viruses are enveloped and susceptible to commonly used antiseptics and surface disinfectants. CONCLUSIONS: Rapid identification of patients with known or suspected infections of these emerging viruses coupled with adherence to infection prevention recommendations will prevent healthcare-associated transmission.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines Monkeypox virus (MPXV), including mpox disease, its clades (clade I, clade II, clade Ib), and associated outbreaks with a focus on nosocomial transmission and infection prevention in healthcare settings.
Evidence base and method
This narrative review synthesizes epidemiology, clinical features, diagnosis, treatment, environmental survival, susceptibility to antiseptics and disinfectants, and infection prevention strategies for MPXV and related orthopoxviruses.
Consensus and conclusions
MPXV demonstrates environmental survival and indirect transmission potential, and as an enveloped virus, it remains susceptible to commonly used antiseptics and surface disinfectants.
Controversies and gaps
The supplied evidence does not report heterogeneity in environmental survival across MPXV clades, nor does it provide comparative data on disinfectant efficacy specifically stratified by clade or environmental conditions.
Research and practice implications
Rapid identification of patients with suspected mpox and strict adherence to infection prevention recommendations are critical to preventing healthcare-associated transmission of MPXV.

过去三十年大流行的发病机制与临床表现:预防、控制与管理证据基础的批判性叙事综述

Epidemiology, Pathophysiology and Clinical Presentation of Pandemics Over the Last Three Decades: A Critical Narrative Review of the Evidence Base for Prevention, Control and Management
作者： Albert Opoku, T. A. Adjei Asafo, Raymond Ahenkorah, Yvonne Arhin Sarpong, A. Adjei
摘要中文翻译过去三十年中的大流行及大流行级别紧急事件产生了数量庞大且异质性显著的证据体系,但流行病学、发病机制与临床文献却鲜少交叉评估,且多以平行方式发展。本综述对该文献进行批判性审视,以人类免疫缺陷病毒大流行为材料,并涵盖2003年严重急性呼吸综合征疫情、2009年甲型H1N1流感大流行、中东呼吸综合征、西非与刚果埃博拉病毒病疫情、寨卡病毒疫情、第七次霍乱大流行、2019冠状病毒病、多国XQTERMAFQXZ紧急事件以及高致病性禽流感A(H5N1)泛动物症传播。文献检索基于三个开放获取书目资源及选定机构来源,所有书目记录均与注册元数据进行核验。综述识别出四个持续存在的问题。第一,大流行的操作性定义仍不稳定,影响了宣布时机、负担估算的可比性及资源分配。第二,指导政策的主要流行病学量化指标(包括基本再生数、病例死亡率及超额XQTERMRQXZ)的估算方法假设常未明示,且不同方法间的分歧足以改变结论。第三,重症发病机制的解释已收敛于少数共享过程,特别是先天免疫失调、内皮损伤与免疫血栓形成,但这些解释的推理基础主要依赖于来自高收入地区重症患者的横断面与尸检材料。第四,对防控措施的证据呈现显著分层,抗病毒药物与若干治疗手段的随机证据较为充分,而非药物干预措施则主要依赖观察性研究与模型证据。信心水平最高的领域包括:严重程度的年龄梯度、若干疫苗平台的有效性以及显著的发病后症状负担;信心水平最低的领域则包括:单项非药物干预措施的比较有效性及发病后综合征的发病机制基础。基于上述发现,研究优先事项包括:标准化负担估算、在严重程度谱系上前瞻性招募机制研究队列以及在紧急事件急性期内能够产出可解释结果的预先商定试验基础设施。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述聚焦过去三十年大流行的发病机制与临床表现,并特别关注XQTERMLQXZ。其目标是对预防、控制与管理策略的证据基础进行批判性评估,涵盖XQTERMAFQXZ及其他类似病毒。
证据基础与方法
本综述基于三个开放获取书目资源及选定机构来源的文献进行综合。所有书目记录均与注册元数据进行核验。本综述为批判性叙事综述而非系统综述或荟萃分析,未对汇总估计或标准化数据分析方法进行显式报告。
共识与结论
本综述识别出文献中四个持续存在的问题:大流行操作性定义的不稳定性、关键流行病学量化指标估算方法的缺乏标准化、对重症发病机制解释的依赖横断面与尸检数据、以及防控措施证据的分层。信心水平最高的领域包括:严重程度的年龄梯度、若干疫苗平台的有效性以及显著的发病后症状负担。
争议与缺口
大流行的操作性定义存在显著争议,影响了宣布时机、负担估算的可比性及资源分配。此外,单项非药物干预措施的比较有效性证据及发病后综合征的发病机制基础证据薄弱。本综述还指出缺乏在严重程度谱系上前瞻性招募的机制研究队列。
科研与实践启示
研究优先事项包括:标准化负担估算方法、在严重程度谱系上前瞻性招募机制研究队列、以及建立能在紧急事件急性期内产出可解释结果的预先商定试验基础设施。这些优先事项旨在提升对XQTERMAFQXZ的理解,并增强预防、控制与管理策略的有效性。

Epidemiology, Pathophysiology and Clinical Presentation of Pandemics Over the Last Three Decades: A Critical Narrative Review of the Evidence Base for Prevention, Control and Management

Authors: Albert Opoku, T. A. Adjei Asafo, Raymond Ahenkorah, Yvonne Arhin Sarpong, A. Adjei
Original abstract (English)
Pandemics and pandemic-scale emergencies of the past three decades have produced an unusually large and heterogeneous body of evidence, yet the epidemiological, mechanistic and clinical literatures have largely developed in parallel and have rarely been appraised together. This review examines that literature critically, taking as its material the human immunodeficiency virus pandemic, the 2003 severe acute respiratory syndrome epidemic, the 2009 influenza A(H1N1) pandemic, Middle East respiratory syndrome, the West African and Congolese Ebola virus disease epidemics, the Zika virus epidemic, the ongoing seventh cholera pandemic, coronavirus disease 2019, the multi-country mpox emergencies, and the panzootic spread of highly pathogenic avian influenza A(H5N1). Literature was identified through searches of three openly accessible bibliographic resources and selected institutional sources, with all bibliographic records verified against registry metadata. The synthesis identifies four persistent problems. First, the operational category of a pandemic remains unstable, which affects the timing of declarations, the comparability of burden estimates and the allocation of resources. Second, the principal epidemiological quantities used to guide policy, including reproduction numbers, case fatality ratios and excess mortality, are estimated by methods whose assumptions are frequently unstated and whose disagreements are substantial enough to alter conclusions. Third, mechanistic accounts of severe disease have converged on a small number of shared processes, in particular innate immune dysregulation, endothelial injury and immunothrombosis, but the inferential basis for these accounts rests heavily on cross-sectional and post-mortem material from severely ill patients in high-income settings. Fourth, evidence on countermeasures is markedly stratified, with randomised evidence for vaccines and several therapeutics but predominantly observational and model-based evidence for non-pharmaceutical interventions. Confidence is strongest for the age gradient of severity, for the efficacy of several vaccine platforms and for the existence of a substantial post-acute symptom burden, and weakest for the comparative effectiveness of individual non-pharmaceutical interventions and for the mechanistic basis of post-acute syndromes. Research priorities that follow from these findings concern standardised burden estimation, prospective mechanistic cohorts recruited across the severity spectrum, and pre-agreed trial infrastructure capable of producing interpretable results during the acute phase of an emergency.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the epidemiology, pathophysiology, and clinical presentation of pandemics over the last three decades, with a particular emphasis on the mpox virus. It aims to critically evaluate the evidence base for prevention, control, and management strategies related to mpox and other similar viruses.
Evidence base and method
The review synthesizes literature from three openly accessible bibliographic resources and selected institutional sources. All bibliographic records were verified against registry metadata. The review is a critical narrative synthesis rather than a systematic or meta-analytic review, as it does not explicitly report on pooled estimates or standardized methodologies for data analysis.
Consensus and conclusions
The review identifies four persistent problems in the literature: the instability of the operational category of a pandemic, the lack of standardized methods for estimating key epidemiological quantities, the reliance on cross-sectional and post-mortem data for mechanistic accounts of severe disease, and the stratification of evidence on countermeasures. Confidence is strongest for the age gradient of severity, the efficacy of several vaccine platforms, and the existence of a substantial post-acute symptom burden.
Controversies and gaps
There is significant controversy regarding the operational definition of a pandemic, which affects the timing of declarations, comparability of burden estimates, and resource allocation. Additionally, the evidence on the comparative effectiveness of individual non-pharmaceutical interventions and the mechanistic basis of post-acute syndromes is weak. The review also highlights a lack of prospective mechanistic cohorts across the severity spectrum.
Research and practice implications
Research priorities include standardizing burden estimation methods, recruiting prospective mechanistic cohorts across the severity spectrum, and establishing pre-agreed trial infrastructure capable of producing interpretable results during the acute phase of an emergency. These priorities aim to improve the understanding of mpox and enhance the effectiveness of prevention, control, and management strategies.

结肠性传播感染——临床表现、内镜特征与实验室诊断:面向全科医师的实用综述

Sexually Transmitted Infections of the Colon—Clinical Picture, Endoscopic Features, and Laboratory Diagnosis: A Practical Review for the General Practitioner
作者： Mariusz Sapuła, Dagny Krankowska, Alicja Wiercińska‐Drapało
摘要中文翻译性传播感染(STIs)是直肠炎和结肠炎的常见且可能被低估的病因。细菌(衣原体、淋病奈瑟菌、梅毒螺旋体、生殖支原体)、病毒(单纯疱疹病毒、猴痘病毒)、阿米巴(溶组织内阿米巴)等病原体可引起炎症性直肠炎或结肠炎,其内镜与组织病理学表现可在不同病原体间模拟炎症性肠病。直肠与结肠肿块罕见,但作为这些感染的重要临床表现,在衣原体、梅毒与大肠杆菌感染中尤为突出。在此背景下,HIV检测至关重要,因其可将机会性病原体纳入鉴别诊断范围。慢性腹泻可为慢性HIV感染的特征。沙门菌属、志贺菌属或弯曲杆菌属等肠道病原体可通过性传播途径传播,尤其是在口-肛接触(“舔肛”)过程中。最常见的STI——人乳头瘤病毒——与结肠炎无关,但因其可引起生殖器疣与肛门癌而备受关注。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述探讨可引起直肠炎或结肠炎的性传播感染(STIs),核心问题聚焦于这些感染的临床、内镜与实验室特征,包括猴痘病毒(MPXV)作为病毒性病因。本综述范围不涵盖非目标病原体,除非存在针对目标病原体的特定证据。
证据基础与方法
本综述为叙述性综合,未报告明确的系统性综述方法、数据库、检索日期或纳入标准。其综合了STIs的一般临床与诊断特征,包括猴痘病毒,但未进行荟萃分析或共识流程。
共识与结论
本综述得出结论:猴痘病毒是直肠炎或结肠炎的已知病毒性病因,可导致炎症性表现并可能模拟炎症性肠病。同时指出,直肠与结肠肿块罕见但作为感染的重要临床表现,包括由猴痘病毒引起的病例。
争议与缺口
本综述未提供猴痘病毒的详细对比数据或解决相关争议。在猴痘病毒诊断准确性、特考韦瑞疗效或猴痘病毒相关结肠炎发病机制等方面缺乏细粒度证据。猴痘病毒分支在疾病严重程度或临床表现中的作用亦未涉及。
科研与实践启示
本综述暗示临床医师应将猴痘病毒纳入直肠炎或结肠炎的鉴别诊断范围,尤其在存在STIs风险因素的患者中。其建议对猴痘病毒与其他STIs病原体进行检测,以实现准确诊断与治疗。

Sexually Transmitted Infections of the Colon—Clinical Picture, Endoscopic Features, and Laboratory Diagnosis: A Practical Review for the General Practitioner

Authors: Mariusz Sapuła, Dagny Krankowska, Alicja Wiercińska‐Drapało
Original abstract (English)
Sexually transmitted infections (STIs) are common and probably underreported causes of proctitis and colitis. Bacterial (chlamydia, gonorrhoea, syphilis, Mycoplasma genitalium), viral (herpes simplex virus, mpox), and amoebic (Entamoeba histolytica) pathogens can cause inflammatory proctitis or colitis, which, depending on the pathogen, can mimic inflammatory bowel disease both on endoscopy and histopathology. Rectal and colonic masses are uncommon, but important manifestations of these infections, especially with chlamydia, syphilis, and E. Testing for HIV is important in this context, since it allows for the inclusion of opportunistic pathogens into the differential diagnosis. Chronic diarrhoea can be a feature of chronic HIV infection. Enteric pathogens, such as Salmonella spp., Shigella spp., or Campylobacter spp., can be transmitted during sex, especially during oral–anal contact (“rimming”). The most common STI, human papillomavirus, is not associated with colitis, but is important because of its causal association with genital warts and anal cancer.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines sexually transmitted infections (STIs) that can cause proctitis or colitis, with a central question on the clinical, endoscopic, and laboratory features of these infections, including mpox virus (MPXV) as a viral cause. The scope excludes non-target pathogens unless target-specific evidence is present.
Evidence base and method
The review is a narrative synthesis without explicit systematic review methods, databases, search dates, or eligibility criteria reported. It synthesizes general clinical and diagnostic features of STIs, including mpox, without meta-analytic or consensus processes.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that mpox virus is a recognized viral cause of proctitis or colitis, contributing to inflammatory presentations that may mimic inflammatory bowel disease. It also notes that rectal and colonic masses are uncommon but important manifestations of infections, including those caused by mpox.
Controversies and gaps
The review does not provide detailed comparative data or resolve controversies specific to mpox virus. It lacks granular evidence on diagnostic accuracy, treatment efficacy, or mechanistic pathways for mpox-associated colitis. The role of mpox clades in disease severity or presentation is not addressed.
Research and practice implications
The review implies that clinicians should include mpox virus in the differential diagnosis of proctitis or colitis, particularly in patients with risk factors for STIs. It suggests that testing for mpox and other STI pathogens is essential for accurate diagnosis and management.

C 补充信息学术文献 · 综述C supplementaryAcademic literature · Review

加强尼日利亚传染病监测:SORMAS和电子综合疾病监测与响应系统的重点范围综述

Strengthening Infectious Disease Surveillance in Nigeria: A Focused Scoping Review of SORMAS and the Electronic Integrated Disease Surveillance and Response System
作者： Ayodele Israel Ajayi, Oluwafemi Emmanuel Ooju, Kennedy Oberhiri Obohwemu, Celestine Ekwuluo, Gordon Mabengban Yakpir, Kenneth Oshiokhayamhe Iyevhobu, Daniel Ikechukwu Oseji, Oluwakemi Laguda-Akingba, Eddy Eidenehi Esezobor, Nera Perpetual Kadiri-Eneh
摘要中文翻译背景 再现性传染病继续在资源有限的环境中对公共卫生构成重大挑战,其中监测系统往往受到基础设施、资金和人力资源的显著限制。在过去十年中,尼日利亚经历了拉沙热、霍乱、黄热病等传染病的反复暴发,凸显了疾病监测、报告和响应方面的持续薄弱环节。数字监测系统越来越多地被引入以加强疫情准备并提高监测绩效,但描述尼日利亚两大主要电子监测平台贡献的证据仍然分散在针对特定疾病的研究和实施研究中。本文综合了关于SORMAS(疫情响应管理与分析系统)和电子综合疾病监测与响应(eIDSR)系统的现有证据,探讨它们在尼日利亚公共卫生系统中对加强传染病监测的贡献。方法 采用重点叙述综合方法,以评估数字工具在尼日利亚管理再现性传染病中的作用。综述遵循已建立的范围综述方法,确定了2015年至2026年间发表的研究。纳入了23项符合条件的出版物,从中综合了与SORMAS和eIDSR相关的具体证据。发现围绕监测绩效、报告及时性、数据质量、疾病检测和实施经验进行组织。结果 证据一致表明,这两个监测系统都增强了尼日利亚的传染病监测能力。SORMAS通过实时报告、电子病例管理、实验室整合和改进的接触追踪,提高了基于病例的监测能力。eIDSR系统加强了常规监测报告,提高了报告完整性,加快了优先疾病的通知,并增强了基层医疗机构与监测当局之间的沟通。尽管有这些进展,实施仍受到基础设施限制、人力资源能力和长期可持续性问题的影响。结论 SORMAS和eIDSR已成为尼日利亚监测架构的重要组成部分,并在多种疫情易发疾病中显著提高了监测绩效。继续投资于基础设施、互操作性、人力资源发展和监测治理对于最大化这些系统在疫情准备和卫生系统韧性的长期贡献至关重要。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述探讨了数字监测系统在尼日利亚加强传染病监测中的作用,重点分析了SORMAS(疫情响应管理与分析系统)和电子综合疾病监测与响应(eIDSR)系统。核心问题关注这些系统如何在尼日利亚公共卫生系统中提升监测绩效、报告及时性、数据质量、疾病检测和实施经验。
证据基础与方法
按照已建立的范围综述方法进行了重点叙述综合。确定了2015年至2026年间发表的研究,并纳入了23项符合条件的出版物。综合了与SORMAS和eIDSR相关的具体证据,以评估它们对监测绩效的影响。
共识与结论
证据一致表明,SORMAS和eIDSR都增强了尼日利亚的传染病监测能力。SORMAS通过实时报告、电子病例管理、实验室整合和改进的接触追踪,提高了基于病例的监测能力。eIDSR系统加强了常规监测报告,提高了报告完整性,加快了优先疾病的通知,并增强了基层医疗机构与监测当局之间的沟通。
争议与缺口
这些系统的实施仍受到基础设施限制、人力资源能力和长期可持续性挑战的影响。综述强调了尽管在数字监测方面取得进展,但疾病监测、报告和响应方面仍存在持续薄弱环节。
科研与实践启示
继续投资于基础设施、互操作性、人力资源发展和监测治理对于最大化SORMAS和eIDSR在尼日利亚疫情准备和卫生系统韧性中的长期贡献至关重要。

Strengthening Infectious Disease Surveillance in Nigeria: A Focused Scoping Review of SORMAS and the Electronic Integrated Disease Surveillance and Response System

Authors: Ayodele Israel Ajayi, Oluwafemi Emmanuel Ooju, Kennedy Oberhiri Obohwemu, Celestine Ekwuluo, Gordon Mabengban Yakpir, Kenneth Oshiokhayamhe Iyevhobu, Daniel Ikechukwu Oseji, Oluwakemi Laguda-Akingba, Eddy Eidenehi Esezobor, Nera Perpetual Kadiri-Eneh
Original abstract (English)
Background Re-emerging infectious diseases continue to present major public health challenges in low-resource settings where surveillance systems often operate under significant infrastructural, financial and workforce constraints. Nigeria has experienced recurrent outbreaks of Lassa fever, cholera, yellow fever and mpox during the past decade, highlighting persistent weaknesses in disease detection, reporting and outbreak response. Digital surveillance systems have increasingly been introduced to strengthen epidemic preparedness and improve surveillance performance, yet evidence describing the contribution of Nigeria's two principal electronic surveillance platforms remains fragmented across disease-specific investigations and implementation studies. This paper synthesises the available evidence on the Surveillance Outbreak Response Management and Analysis System (SORMAS) and the electronic Integrated Disease Surveillance and Response (eIDSR) system, examining their contribution to strengthening infectious disease surveillance within Nigeria's public health system. Methods A focused narrative synthesis was undertaken to examine digital tools for managing re-emerging infectious diseases in Nigeria. The review followed established scoping review methodology and identified studies published between 2015 and 2026. Twenty-three eligible publications were included, from which evidence relating specifically to SORMAS and eIDSR was synthesised. Findings were organised around surveillance performance, reporting timeliness, data quality, outbreak detection and implementation experiences. Results The evidence consistently demonstrates that both surveillance systems have strengthened infectious disease surveillance in Nigeria. SORMAS has enhanced case-based surveillance through real-time reporting, electronic case management, laboratory integration and improved contact tracing. The eIDSR system has strengthened routine surveillance reporting, improved reporting completeness, accelerated notification of priority diseases and enhanced communication between peripheral health facilities and surveillance authorities. Despite these advances, implementation continues to be influenced by infrastructure limitations, workforce capacity and long-term sustainability. Conclusion SORMAS and eIDSR have become integral components of Nigeria's surveillance architecture and have substantially improved surveillance performance across several epidemic-prone diseases. Continued investment in infrastructure, interoperability, workforce development and surveillance governance will be essential to maximise their long-term contribution to epidemic preparedness and health system resilience.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review examines the role of digital surveillance systems in strengthening infectious disease surveillance in Nigeria, focusing on the Surveillance Outbreak Response Management and Analysis System (SORMAS) and the electronic Integrated Disease Surveillance and Response (eIDSR) system. The central question addresses how these systems contribute to surveillance performance, reporting timeliness, data quality, outbreak detection, and implementation experiences within Nigeria's public health system.
Evidence base and method
A focused narrative synthesis was conducted following established scoping review methodology. Studies published between 2015 and 2026 were identified, and 23 eligible publications were included. Evidence specific to SORMAS and eIDSR was synthesized to assess their impact on surveillance performance.
Consensus and conclusions
The evidence consistently demonstrates that both SORMAS and eIDSR have strengthened infectious disease surveillance in Nigeria. SORMAS enhanced case-based surveillance through real-time reporting, electronic case management, laboratory integration, and improved contact tracing. The eIDSR system strengthened routine surveillance reporting, improved reporting completeness, accelerated notification of priority diseases, and enhanced communication between peripheral health facilities and surveillance authorities.
Controversies and gaps
Implementation of these systems continues to be influenced by infrastructure limitations, workforce capacity, and long-term sustainability challenges. The review highlights persistent weaknesses in disease detection, reporting, and outbreak response despite advances in digital surveillance.
Research and practice implications
Continued investment in infrastructure, interoperability, workforce development, and surveillance governance is essential to maximize the long-term contribution of SORMAS and eIDSR to epidemic preparedness and health system resilience in Nigeria.

🚨 突发动态与新闻 / Health News

Published: 2026-08-19  |  Publisher: Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)
A 高优先级公共卫生新闻

猴痘病毒I型和II型分支的临床概述 - 美国疾病控制与预防中心 | CDC (.gov)

Clinical Overview of Clade I and Clade II Monkeypox - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)
新闻简报美国疾病控制与预防中心(CDC)于2026年8月19日发布了猴痘病毒(MPXV)的临床概述。该文件重点介绍了MPXV的两个主要分支——I型和II型,它们具有不同的地理分布和传播模式。I型猴痘病毒暴发始于2024年在中非地区,并通过旅行在全球范围内传播;II型猴痘病毒暴发始于2022年,目前在全球100多个国家呈地方性流行。两种分支均可通过密切身体接触(包括性接触)传播,且治疗和预防方法相似。然而,I型历史上病死率较高,但在最佳护理下近期数据显示其病死率较低。II型病死率较低,死亡率低于0.1%。CDC建议采用疫苗接种进行预防和暴露后预防,同时需详细询问患者病史并给予支持性治疗。免疫功能低下人群(包括营养不良儿童)重症风险较高。CDC还强调对疑似猴痘患者进行HIV检测的重要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
CDC于2026年8月19日发布了该临床概述。该文件讨论了历史和持续的猴痘病毒暴发,其中I型猴痘病毒暴发始于2024年,II型猴痘病毒暴发始于2022年。
地点与对象
猴痘病毒暴发主要位于中非和东非地区,旅行相关病例已在美洲、欧洲和世界其他地区报告。受影响人群包括成人和儿童,全球猴痘暴发中男男性行为者以及免疫功能低下人群是重点关注对象。
事件
CDC提供了猴痘病毒的临床概述,特别强调了I型和II型猴痘病毒分支的差异。文件指出,两种分支的传播、治疗和预防方法相似,但其病死率和传播模式可能存在差异。该文件还为医护人员提供了临床检测、诊断和预防的指导。
规模、影响与风险
I型猴痘病毒暴发始于2024年,目前在中非和东非地区持续流行,旅行相关病例已在全球报告。II型猴痘病毒暴发始于2022年,已传播至100多个非流行国家。I型病死率≤2.5%,II型病死率低于0.1%。免疫功能低下人群(包括营养不良儿童和其他健康状况不佳者)的病死率更高。
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC概述了临床检测、诊断和预防措施,包括使用特考韦瑞进行暴露后预防。文件强调详细询问患者病史、旅行史和性史,以及给予支持性治疗的重要性。文件还提到,猴痘病毒可通过密切皮肤接触、性接触以及母婴垂直传播传播,医护人员还可能因锐器伤或间接接触传播而面临感染风险。
A high priorityPublic-health news

Clinical Overview of Clade I and Clade II Monkeypox - Centers for Disease Control and Prevention | CDC (.gov)

News Brief
The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) published a clinical overview of the monkeypox virus (MPXV) on August 19, 2026. The document highlights the two main clades of MPXV, clade I and clade II, which have different geographic distributions and transmission patterns. Clade I outbreaks began in Central Africa in 2024 and have spread globally through travel, while clade II outbreaks started in 2022 and are now endemic in over 100 countries. Both clades can be transmitted through close physical contact, including sexual contact, and can be treated and prevented similarly. However, clade I historically had a higher case-fatality rate, though recent data show it to be low with optimal care. Clade II has a lower fatality rate, with less than 0.1% mortality. The CDC recommends vaccines for prevention and post-exposure prophylaxis, along with thorough patient history and supportive care. Immunocompromised individuals, including children with malnutrition, are at higher risk of severe disease. The CDC also emphasizes the importance of testing for HIV in individuals suspected of having monkeypox.

Key points Regardless of clade, monkeypox can be spread, treated, and prevented the same way. Consider monkeypox as the cause of a diffuse or localized rash, particularly if there's recent travel to an outbreak area; evaluate any individual presenting with certain ulcers or rash for HIV and STIs. Conduct a thorough patient history, including detailed travel and sexual histories, to assess possible exposure to monkeypox. Provide patients with supportive care and pain control early in the illness. While there are no FDA-approved treatments specifically for monkeypox, people who have or are at risk for severe disease may benefit from additional care. Vaccine is approved and recommended for the prevention of monkeypox and can be used for post-exposure prophylaxis. Overview There are two types of the monkeypox virus (MPXV), clade I and clade II, each with two known subclades (Ia, Ib, IIa, IIb). Both clades and all subclades can be spread, treated, and prevented similarly. Historically, clade I MPXV was reported to have a much higher case-fatality rate than clade II MPXV; however, data from recent outbreaks suggest that the case-fatality rate for both clades is low, especially when optimal supportive care is provided. Risk factors, affected populations, and locations of sustained transmission can differ between clades. A large outbreak of clade I MPXV began in 2024 and continues throughout Central and Eastern Africa. There also have been travel-associated clade I MPXV cases reported worldwide, including the United States. Community transmission has been identified in certain parts of Europe and the United States. A global clade II MPXV outbreak began in 2022, and cases continue to spread at low levels in many countries around the world. MPXV is usually transmitted from person to person through close, sustained physical contact. In the clade I outbreak originating in Central Africa, transmission has occurred through sexual contact, day-to-day household contact, and in healthcare settings when PPE was not available. In the ongoing clade II MPXV outbreak, transmission has been almost exclusively associated with sexual contact More information about each MPXV subclade More about monkeypox clades and subclades based on what we know at this time Clade la Clade lb Clade Ila Clade llb Geography Endemic to Central Africa, including Republic of the Congo, Gabon, Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), Central African Republic, and southeastern Cameroon Newly identified in the DRC with spread to nearby countries; travel-associated cases around the world Endemic to West Africa, including northwestern Cameroon, Nigeria, Ghana, Cote d'Ivoire, Liberia, and Sierra Leone. Endemic to West Africa (identified originally in Nigeria); spread to more than 100 non-endemic countries as part of the ongoing global outbreak that began in 2022 Current Situation Outbreak in Central Africa from 2023 to present Outbreak in Central and Eastern Africa. There have been travel-associated cases in other parts of Africa, Asia, Europe, and North America, including the United States Insufficient data Caused the ongoing global monkeypox outbreak that began in 2022; still circulating globally at low levels, including in the United States Population Primarily Affected in Outbreaks since 2022 Both adults and children Adults (often sex workers and their contacts); subsequent spread through day-to-day household contact Both adults and children Adults, particularly men who have sex with men in global outbreak What's Known about How it's Spread Primarily by contact with infected live or dead wild animals. Transmission can also occur via mother to fetus or close skin-to-skin contact, including intimate (e.g., massage, kissing) and sexual contact. Primarily via close skin-to-skin contact, including intimate (e.g., massage, kissing) and sexual contact. Transmission can also occur via mother to fetus, or within households. Primarily by contact with infected live or dead wild animals. Transmission can also occur via mother to fetus or close skin-to-skin contact, including intimate (e.g., massage, kissing) and sexual contact. Primarily via close skin-to-skin contact, including intimate (e.g., massage, kissing) and sexual contact. Transmission can also occur via mother to fetus, or within households. Transmission to healthcare workers from sharps injuries and fomite transmission are rare but have been documented. Mortality Rate From more recent outbreaks, the mortality rate is ≤ 2.5%. Most deaths occur in people with immunocompromising conditions including children with malnutrition and other health conditions. Mortality rate is less than 0.5% in Central and Eastern Africa. Most deaths occur in people with immunocompromising conditions. No mortality has been seen with travel associated cases outside Africa, but data are limited. Mortality rate is around 1% but has limited data. Available data suggest lower CFR than clade I monkeypox Mortality rate is less than 0.1%. Most deaths occur in people with immunocompromising conditions. Vaccine 2 doses of JYNNEOS vaccine; 1 dose of ACAM2000 for specific populations Other Prevention Considerations Avoid direct or skin-to-skin contact with people who have a rash that looks like monkeypox, including during sex or intimate contact; avoid objects or materials a person with monkeypox has used; avoid wild animals and animal products (lotions, bushmeat, etc.) in areas where monkeypox occurs regularly; wash hands regularly Treatment Healthcare professionals should assess pain in all patients with monkeypox and recognize that substantial pain may exist from mucosal lesions not evident on physical exam. Additional treatments may be needed for people at higher risk of severe disease. Other Therapeutic Considerations Most people recover with supportive care (nutritious food, fluids, antibiotics for secondary skin infections) and pain control (over-the-counter medications like acetaminophen and ibuprofen, topical steroids and anesthetics like lidocaine for local pain relief; prescription pain medications for short-term management of severe pain). Evaluating patients with suspected monkeypox Patient history and physical examination Clinicians should consider monkeypox when determining the cause of a diffuse or localized rash. Other diseases that can present similarly to monkeypox include herpes simplex virus (genital herpes), syphilis, herpes zoster (shingles), disseminated varicella-zoster virus infection, molluscum contagiosum, scabies, lymphogranuloma venereum, allergic skin rashes, and drug eruptions. Clinicians should conduct a thorough patient history to assess possible monkeypox exposures or epidemiologic risk factors, including a detailed sexual history and travel history for any patient with suspected monkeypox. Perform a complete physical examination, including a thorough skin and mucosal (e.g., oral, genital, anal) examination for the characteristic vesiculo-pustular rash of monkeypox. Clinicians may detect lesions of which the patient may be unaware, especially in the anogenital area. If you suspect monkeypox Follow recommendations for monkeypox infection prevention and control in healthcare settings. Additional HIV/STI considerations for patients with suspected or confirmed monkeypox In the ongoing clade II monkeypox outbreak, HIV infection and other STIs have been highly prevalent among people with monkeypox. Furthermore, people with HIV-associated immunocompromise are at risk for severe manifestations of monkeypox. Evaluate any individual presenting with genital, anal, or perianal ulcers, proctitis, or diffuse rash for STIs per the CDC STI Treatment Guidelines. All sexually active adults and adolescents in whom monkeypox is suspected should be evaluated for HIV and other STIs. Offer appropriate care to those with positive test results. The diagnosis of another STI does not exclude monkeypox as a concurrent infection may be present. Test for other STIs including syphilis, gonorrhea, and chlamydia in every sexually active adult and adolescent in whom monkeypox is suspected or confirmed. Test for HIV in every sexually active adult and adolescent in whom monkeypox is suspected or confirmed if current HIV status is unknown. Ensure those with HIV and with suspected or confirmed monkeypox are on effective antiretroviral therapy and linked to care to optimize immune function. Discuss and facilitate access to HIV pre-exposure prophylaxis (PrEP) for those who are HIV negative and at risk for HIV. Instruct patients with suspected monkeypox to follow isolation recommendations and avoid close contact with other people and with animals including pets until monkeypox has resolved. CDC’s Sexually Transmitted Infections (STI) Treatment Guidelines provide current evidence-based prevention, diagnostic, and treatment recommendations for clinicians. Always assess patients based on your clinical circumstances and local disease burden. Additional considerations for people at risk for severe monkeypox Most people with monkeypox will recover within weeks with supportive care. Patients who are severely immunocompromised or have certain skin conditions are at particular risk of uncontrolled viral spread, which can cause severe manifestations of monkeypox and even be life-threatening. People who are at risk for severe monkeypox caused by either clade include those who: Are severely immunocompromised (e.g., those with HIV with CD4 <200, or solid organ transplant recipients) Have atopic dermatitis and other conditions affecting skin integrity Are children Are pregnant or breastfeeding CDC has interim clinical guidance that may assist clinicians in managing patients with or who are at risk for severe monkeypox. Treatment for these patients involves Food and Drug Administration (FDA)–regulated drugs that are primarily stockpiled by the U.S. government for smallpox preparedness. Resource If you suspect monkeypox in a patient, follow CDC recommendations for monkeypox infection prevention and control in healthcare settings. Prevention Monkeypox vaccine CDC routinely recommends vaccination for people with sexual risk factors who have not been diagnosed with monkeypox during the ongoing outbreak or have not already received 2 doses of the JYNNEOS vaccine. People at risk for monkeypox (regardless of clade) ideally should be vaccinated prior to exposure to MPXV. People may receive MPXV post-exposure prophylaxis to help prevent monkeypox (post-exposure prophylaxis). Two vaccines may be used for the prevention of monkeypox: JYNNEOS vaccine is FDA-approved and recommended by CDC and ACIP for the prevention of monkeypox and smallpox. It is available through the commercial supply chain. ACAM2000 vaccine is FDA-approved for immunization against smallpox and MPXV. In the United States, there is a large supply of ACAM2000, but this vaccine has more known side effects and contraindications. The standard regimen for JYNNEOS involves a subcutaneous (SQ) route of administration with an injection volume of 0.5mL. An alternative regimen involving intradermal (ID) administration with an injection volume of 0.1mL may be used under an Emergency Use Authorization (EUA). Both the standard (0.5mL SQ) and the alternative (0.1mL ID) regimen have been found to be effective for monkeypox prevention. There is adequate supply of JYNNEOS vaccine. Therefore, clinicians can preferentially administer JYNNEOS via the subcutaneous route. JYNNEOS vaccine is licensed as a series of two doses administered 28 days (4 weeks) apart. People who are vaccinated should continue to take steps to protect themselves from infection by avoiding close, skin-to-skin contact, including intimate contact, with someone who has monkeypox. Keep Reading Health Alert Network (HAN) 00516 - Prevention Strategies for Monkeypox, including Vaccinating People at Risk via Sexual Exposure, for U.S. Travelers Visiting Countries with Clade I Monkeypox Outbreaks Additional doses of vaccine It’s not currently recommended for most people to have more than 2 doses of JYNNEOS vaccine. However, for those at occupational risk of exposure to orthopoxviruses (e.g., certain research laboratorians*), administration of additional doses is recommended at two- to 10-year intervals depending on the type of work being performed and vaccine used. *Research laboratory personnel are those who directly handle cultures or animals contaminated or infected with monkeypox virus (MPXV). Vaccination is not routinely recommended for clinical laboratory personnel who perform routine chemistry, hematology, and urinalysis testing, including for patients with suspected or confirmed MPXV infection, healthcare personnel who care for patients with monkeypox or administer ACAM2000. Recommended infection prevention and control practices are effective in minimizing transmission. Vaccination can be offered based on site- and activity-specific biosafety risk assessments (e.g., identification of laboratory procedures with a high likelihood of generating aerosols or inadequate PPE availability). Counseling message on condoms It is not known whether condoms prevent the transmission of monkeypox. If rashes are confined to the genitals or anus, condoms may help. However, since infectious respiratory secretions may be present, condoms alone are probably not enough to prevent monkeypox. Condoms are effective at preventing the transmission of some infections, such as chlamydia, gonorrhea, and HIV. International health organizations advise that people with monkeypox use condoms for 12 weeks after they recover until more is known about levels of the virus and potential infectivity in semen during the period that follows recovery. MPXV testing Obtain specimens for testing from accessible lesions, including those inside the mouth, anus, or vagina. Keep Reading Collecting and Handling Specimens for Monkeypox PCR Testing Patient management Treatment All patients with monkeypox may benefit from supportive care and pain control begun early in the illness. Monkeypox can commonly cause severe pain and can affect vulnerable anatomic sites, including the genitals and oropharynx, which can lead to other complications. Assess pain in all patients with monkeypox and recognize that substantial pain may exist from mucosal lesions not evident on physical exam. Use topical and systemic strategies to manage pain. Pain management strategies should be patient-centered and tailored to the needs and context of an individual patient. However, some patients experience severe manifestations of monkeypox. Consider additional monkeypox-directed treatment in people who have severe disease or risk factors for severe disease. Currently there is no FDA-approved treatment specifically for monkeypox; treatment involves Food and Drug Administration (FDA)–regulated medications that are primarily stockpiled by the U.S. government for smallpox preparedness. Resource CDC has interim clinical guidance to assist clinicians in managing patients with or at risk for severe monkeypox. CDC poxvirus staff are available Monday-Friday, 8 a.m.-4 p.m. ET, for clinical consults for patients with severe monkeypox disease by calling the CDC Emergency Operations Center at 770-488-7100 or emailing poxvirus@cdc.gov. View five news elements
Time
The CDC published this clinical overview on August 19, 2026. The document discusses historical and ongoing outbreaks, with the clade I outbreak beginning in 2024 and the clade II outbreak starting in 2022.
Location and population
The outbreaks are primarily located in Central and Eastern Africa, with travel-associated cases reported in the United States, Europe, and other parts of the world. The affected populations include adults and children, with a focus on men who have sex with men in the global outbreak and immunocompromised individuals.
Event
The CDC provides a clinical overview of the monkeypox virus, specifically highlighting the differences between clade I and clade II MPXV. It notes that both clades can be spread, treated, and prevented similarly, but their case-fatality rates and transmission patterns may vary. The document also outlines guidance for healthcare providers on diagnosis, treatment, and prevention.
Scale, impact and risk
The clade I outbreak began in 2024 and continues in Central and Eastern Africa, with travel-associated cases reported globally. The clade II outbreak started in 2022 and has spread to over 100 non-endemic countries. The case-fatality rate for clade I is ≤ 2.5%, while for clade II it is less than 0.1%. Mortality is higher in immunocompromised individuals, including children with malnutrition and other health conditions.
Response, status and uncertainty
The CDC outlines clinical testing, diagnosis, and prevention measures, including the use of vaccines for post-exposure prophylaxis. It emphasizes the importance of patient history, travel and sexual histories, and supportive care. The document also mentions that transmission can occur through close skin-to-skin contact, sexual contact, and from mother to fetus, with healthcare workers at risk from sharps injuries and fomite transmission.

Is mpox here to stay? CDC study says virus is likely becoming endemic - CIDRAP

News Brief
The article, published on August 21, 2026, by CIDRAP, discusses a CDC study indicating that the mpox virus is likely becoming endemic in the United States. The study highlights the potential for ongoing transmission but does not provide specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers. The analysis is based on broader epidemiological trends rather than a confirmed outbreak. The CDC is conducting research to understand these trends, and the findings suggest that mpox may persist in the population. The article does not report any specific public health response measures or containment efforts, as the study is still under investigation. The implications of the study focus on the long-term presence of mpox, rather than immediate health risks or outbreaks.

Clade I Mpox Transmission Risk During Air Travel Appears Low - Infectious Disease Special Edition

News Brief
The CDC has reported that the transmission risk for clade I monkeypox virus (MPXV) during commercial air travel is very low, based on an analysis of three infected travelers who flew into or within the United States while infectious. The findings were published on August 22, 2026, and highlight the importance of understanding viral transmission dynamics in travel settings. The report does not provide specific case or death counts but emphasizes the low risk level. The analysis was conducted in 2023 and 2024, with no further details on investigation, testing, or response measures mentioned. This information is crucial for public health planning and travel advisories, ensuring that appropriate precautions are taken without unnecessary alarm.

几内亚比绍疑似猴痘病例中儿童占一半 - streamlinefeed.co.ke

Children Account for Half of Guinea-Bissau Suspected Mpox Cases - streamlinefeed.co.ke
新闻简报几内亚比绍正经历其首次猴痘暴发,截至2026年8月17日已报告46例疑似病例。联合国儿童基金会(UNICEF)强调,这些疑似病例中有23例为15岁以下儿童,其中16例年龄在0至4岁之间,该年龄段儿童面临严重并发症的更高风险。暴发于2026年7月4日被宣布,已确诊7例成人病例,但尚无死亡报告。该国的监测系统被描述为极其脆弱,引发对未被发现的传播传播链的担忧。UNICEF正与公共卫生部及世界卫生组织合作,加强基于社区的监测、接触者追踪及感染预防措施。当前局势凸显在已面临其他健康危机的地区管理猴痘的挑战,并强调早期检测与公众意识的必要性以防止进一步传播。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Children Account for Half of Guinea-Bissau Suspected Mpox Cases - streamlinefeed.co.ke

News Brief
Guinea-Bissau is experiencing its first mpox outbreak, with 46 suspected cases reported as of 17 August 2026. UNICEF highlighted that 23 of these suspected cases involve children under 15, with 16 specifically aged 0 to 4 years, who are at higher risk for severe complications. The outbreak was declared on 4 July 2026, and seven confirmed adult cases have been identified, though no deaths have been reported. The country's surveillance system is described as extremely fragile, raising concerns about undetected transmission chains. UNICEF is collaborating with the Ministry of Public Health and WHO to enhance community-based surveillance, contact tracing, and infection prevention measures. The situation underscores the challenges of managing mpox in a region already dealing with other health crises, and emphasizes the need for early detection and public awareness to prevent further spread.

Learn more Build your reading streak0/3 today·Counts at 25% UNICEF warned that 23 of Guinea-Bissau's 46 suspected mpox cases are children under 15, as the country confronts the first mpox outbreak in its recorded history. The outbreak, declared on 4 July 2026, has so far produced seven confirmed cases, all among adults, yet the age profile of suspected cases points to silent transmission among the youngest and most vulnerable. With schools reopening in September, West African health authorities face a familiar dilemma: a virus moving faster than the surveillance systems built to catch it. What Mpox Is and Why It Matters Now Mpox is an orthopoxvirus that spreads through close physical contact, producing fever, swollen lymph nodes and a distinctive rash. Africa has faced two epidemic waves in recent years: clade I centred on the Democratic Republic of Congo, and clade II long circulating in Nigeria, where outbreaks have been recorded since 2017. Guinea-Bissau's emergence as a first-time responder therefore lands in a continent already stretched by concurrent emergencies. An Outbreak Without Visible Chains Six of the confirmed cases sit in the Autonomous Sector of Bissau and one in Biombo Region, and none appear connected to each other. For UNICEF that disconnection is the alarm: unlinked cases imply undetected chains of transmission, meaning the true caseload may be running ahead of the register. No deaths have been reported to date, but the confirmed adult cases sit atop 46 suspected infections, and the gap between suspicion and confirmation is where the virus hides. 4 July 2026: first mpox outbreak declared in Guinea-Bissau 7 confirmed cases as of 17 August, all adults, none fatal 46 suspected cases, of which 23 are children under 15 16 suspected cases in children aged 0 to 4 years Why Children Carry the Risk Sixteen of the 23 suspected child cases are aged 0 to 4, the group most exposed to severe complications. Sandra Martins, UNICEF Deputy Representative in Guinea-Bissau, said the country's surveillance system remains extremely fragile and that cases, particularly among children, risk going undetected. The September reopening of schools compounds the concern, because classrooms multiply the close contacts through which mpox spreads. Martins urged families to seek care immediately if a child develops a rash, fever or swollen lymph nodes, and to follow guidance on handwashing and avoiding close contact with suspected cases. Stigma is part of the clinical picture. Because visible rashes invite exclusion, UNICEF is pairing surveillance with risk communication so that families report symptoms early instead of hiding them, and so that children who recover are not marked when they return to class. Patching a Fragile Surveillance System UNICEF is working with the Ministry of Public Health and WHO to strengthen community-based surveillance, contact tracing and sample testing, and to reinforce infection prevention, water and sanitation measures in health facilities. The critical gaps, the agency says, sit precisely in identifying contacts and tracing how the virus moves. For readers across the continent the outbreak is a reminder of mpox's wider African geography: Nigeria has managed recurrent clade II outbreaks since 2017, and the Democratic Republic of Congo is simultaneously battling an Ebola epidemic that has strained regional alert systems. Port health units from Lagos to Mombasa treat every new West African signal as a cue to recheck screening protocols. "With early detection, care and prevention, mpox can be managed and its spread reduced," Martins said. In Bissau's clinics, early is the word that now does the work. How did this story land? B routine priorityPublic-health news

Children Make Up Half of Suspected Mpox Cases in Guinea-Bissau - Devdiscourse

News Brief
Guinea-Bissau is experiencing its first recorded mpox outbreak, with 46 suspected cases reported as of 17 August 2026. Among these, 23 involve children under 15, and 16 of those are between birth and four years old, raising concerns about the potential for serious complications. No deaths have been confirmed, but the lack of apparent links between cases suggests possible hidden transmission chains. The country's health surveillance system is described as fragile, with gaps in community-level case detection, contact identification, and systematic tracing. UNICEF has increased support for public health efforts, including strengthening disease surveillance, contact tracing, and infection prevention measures. The upcoming school reopening in September adds another challenge, as children returning to classrooms may increase transmission risks if infections are not identified quickly. Health officials are urging families to seek care if symptoms such as a rash, fever, or swollen lymph nodes appear and to follow public health advice on handwashing and avoiding close contact with suspected cases. The outbreak highlights the need for improved surveillance and community awareness to protect vulnerable populations, particularly children.
